# Far Cry 3 — co-op notes

Russian version: [NOTES.ru.md](NOTES.ru.md)

Status: active (owner decision 2026-10-22). Plan: [PLAN.md](PLAN.md).

## Game identity (AGENTS.md rule 5)

Observed 2026-10-22 on the owner's PC:

- Store: Steam, app 220240, `E:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\common\Far Cry 3`; runs through Ubisoft Connect (`bin\upc_r1_loader.dll`), no `steam_api.dll`
- Steam build id: 13146413
- SHA-256 `bin\farcry3.exe`: `fd3929f19ff825912f73bd0158604823c46ab9c124d18d57b0fab05a51cda9bb`
- SHA-256 `bin\farcry3_d3d11.exe`: `5437b2e37a54f8a3b28c342f2b2670ca907fd60bc74905b0b7527f69bafc965e`
- SHA-256 `bin\FC3.dll`: `71d58a7a4503c5c67dcd6f4b9445b5e7ac34c12043987f4c7285e13f2ed51c9b`
- SHA-256 `bin\FC3_d3d11.dll`: `e541594e11ac6091dc7d3702f2cd90504280b82cf15ec10b4eb148aae27f0809`
- Engine: Dunia
- Install checked for third-party DLLs: `bin` holds only the game's own files
- `data_win32\patch.dat/.fat` held the owner's auto-skip-cutscenes mod. With the owner's permission it was renamed to `patch.dat.skipcutscenes` / `patch.fat.skipcutscenes`, and the vanilla pair (kept as `.vanilla`) was put back. SHA-256 vanilla `patch.dat`: `b76a3ae5092bec46e4d000dfebb0dc1fac9d7990d217421528447d59a7661833`, `patch.fat`: `39dc4178723bfd4e2f9f512d42401790b80a3f4ea305d4ab38889f546780b39f`
- Also present: `bin\FC3Editor.exe` (official map editor), `data_win32\worlds\fc3_main`, `data_win32\worlds\multicommon`

## Known so far

- Official co-op: separate 6-mission campaign, up to 4 players online https://co-optimus.com/article/8473/far-cry-3-follow-up-questions-plus-new-co-op-campaign-trailer-and-images.html . Mod: Open World Co-op (scubrah), no story missions, alpha https://www.nexusmods.com/farcry3/mods/220 (coop-anything directory).

## Phase 1: unpacking the game data (2026-10-24)

- Tool: Gibbed.Dunia (https://github.com/Gibbed/Gibbed.Dunia, commit a43405c), built from source with a portable .NET SDK 8.0.426 (`Tools\dotnet-sdk-8.0.426`, archive SHA-256 `b2523f881c97e13234e9b3905fe9978831b84e52f8115ba397d31203d2e022ce`, verified against SHA-512 from Microsoft release metadata). Clone: `Downloads\gibbed-dunia\src`, build output `bin_fc3\Gibbed.FarCry3.Unpack.exe`.
- The upstream repo has no Far Cry 3 unpacker, but `BigFileV2_64` already knows FAT version 9 (Windows, compression version 3). Local patch [docs/gibbed-dunia-fc3.patch](docs/gibbed-dunia-fc3.patch) adds: project `Gibbed.FarCry3.Unpack`, config `configs/Far Cry 3`, FAT v9 compression scheme mapping (0 none, 1 LZO1x, 2 Zlib), and a cap on LZO output for the 64-byte type guess.
- Result in `Work\fc3`: `patch.fat` gives 427 files (388 Lua, 11 bin, 6 xbg, 5 hkx, 2 seq); `common.fat` gives 3099 files (1984 xbt, 184 feu, 89 xml, 58 fcb). No name lists exist for FC3, so files are named by hash.
- Mission scripts are plain Lua text generated by the "Domino" tool (`-- {\v/} Domino auto-generated LUA script file`, header names the source document, e.g. `domino/library/sp/lib_mission.domino.xml`).
- Next: unpack `worlds\fc3_main` and the remaining archives, group the Domino scripts by source document, find mission and collectible tables.

### Phase 1 findings: `fc3_main` (2026-10-24)

- `worlds\fc3_main\fc3_main.fat` unpacks to `Work\fc3\fc3_main`: 2.7 GB; 17944 xbt, 5247 xbg, 3628 bin, 3397 hkx, 2229 lua, 1069 fcb, 818 seq. The FAT header fields at 0x0C/0x10 (117303 and 50 here) belong to locale tables after the main table; the main table (64354 entries) alone covers the whole `.dat` (max offset + size = file size). Local patch skips the locale tables. Locale and `multicommon` archives are not unpacked yet.
- Script index (script file to source Domino document): `Work\fc3\script-index.tsv`, 2363 lines. Groups: main missions 1175 scripts (`fc3_mainmissions/<id>`, about 80 mission ids: `jas_*` Jason, `hoy_*` Hoyt, `doc_*` Doc, `bam_*`, `cit_*`, `den_*`, `hur_*`, `sam_*`, `wil_*`, `bng_*`, `game_chapter02..10`, `game_credits`), side quests 155 (`fc3_poisquests`), encounters 103, social challenges 82, quest givers 82, hub/chain quests 81, DLC ulc 74, outposts 33 (`fc3_outpost/<grid cell>`), persistency 21 (`fc3_persistency`), activities 14 (poker, knife throw, races, shooting gallery, shop), masters 91 (`fc3_master`: outposts, radio towers, persistency, teleport, narrative scenes, restricted zones, side quests).
- The Lua files are only Domino graphs: they create and wire "boxes" (`Domino/System/AI/`, `Player/`, `Narrative/`, `UI/` and so on). The box code is not in Lua; it is native code in `FC3.dll`. So mission and progress logic can be changed in two places: the graphs (our own archive) and the native boxes (ASI hooks).
- The game already has co-op boxes: `Domino/System/Coop/` (AllPlayers: GodMode, UnlimitedAmmo, TeleportPlayer; Group: AddToGroup, GetFromGroup, GroupUseSTP; Misc: GetEntityByName, EndGame) and the library `domino/library/coop/helpers`. They are used by the 6 official co-op missions in `multicommon`, not by the single-player campaign.
- Consequence for the variant choice (A: engine network layer, B: own ASI network): the single-player graphs have no multi-player awareness, so a decision needs the native side (`FC3.dll`) in REA/Ghidra and the scubrah mod. REA did not connect at session start (CONNECT_TIMEOUT).

### Phase 1 findings: `multicommon` and the co-op boxes (2026-10-24)

- `worlds\multicommon\multicommon.fat` unpacks to `Work\fc3\multicommon` (2128 Lua scripts; its documents are appended to `script-index.tsv`). It holds the 6 official co-op missions as Domino documents `domino/user/coop/coop_{vidar,huginn,skadi,freyja,sleipnir,fenrir}` (about 1650 scripts), a race mode `coop_cce_race` and the shared libraries `domino/library/coop/{mission,pawns,vehicle,mapsetup,ui,helpers}`.
- Co-op boxes are for small objective missions, not for the story: `PlayerCount` / `PlayerCountTest` (3178 uses), `AllPlayers/*` (GodMode, TeleportPlayer, ProximityTrigger, ManageInventoryAllPlayers), `SetCoopCheckPoint`, `Group*`, `StrayPlayerZone`, `CoopGate`, `ForceOutPlayerFromVehicle`. Reusable idea: the engine already counts players, teleports all players, and keeps a shared co-op checkpoint.
- Single-player graphs reach the player through one box: `Domino/System/Player/GetLocalPlayer` (1484 uses in `fc3_main`, 291 in `patch`, only 10 in `multicommon`). Other player boxes (`ManageInventory_v4` 197, `SetHudMode` 136, `PostFx` 637, `ForceInVehicle`, `SwitchPawnPlayer`) take that player as input. Candidate hook for variant B: the native code of `GetLocalPlayer` and the player-slot lookup `GetPlayerFromSlot`; for variant A: how `PlayerCountTest` reads the player list.
- Next: find these boxes in `FC3.dll` (REA/Ghidra, strings `GetLocalPlayer`, `PlayerCount`), then try to start the open world inside a co-op session. (Done below: the boxes are Lua, not native code.)

### Phase 1 findings: `FC3.dll` and the Domino boxes (2026-10-24)

- **Correction:** the box code IS Lua. A graph loads boxes with `cbox:RegisterBox("Domino/System/Player/GetLocalPlayer.lua")`; each box is a Lua file in the same archives, with a reflection header (`-- DOMINO REFLECTION BOX START`, `<Display>`, `<ControlIn>`, `<DataOut>`). The earlier claim "box code is native in `FC3.dll`" was wrong. Only the Lua globals the boxes call (for example `GetLocalPlayerId()`) are native.
- **File names are hashes of box names.** Name hash = CRC-64/ISO (reflected, polynomial 0xD800000000000000, init 0, no final xor, table = `Gibbed.Dunia.FileFormats.Hashing.CRC64`) over the **lower-cased path with backslashes**. Check: `domino\system\player\getlocalplayer.lua` gives `9716CA537E634631`, which exists in `fc3_main\__UNKNOWN\unknown\` and `multicommon\__UNKNOWN\unknown\` (782 bytes, identical in both, MD5 `5E8EFF68D5AF85A2E7F849D233170134`). Also `domino\system\getscriptentity.lua` gives `235DF3F86557C6FA`. Files without extension in `unknown\` are the boxes.
- **Name lists exist after all:** the resource manifests `__UNKNOWN\misc\*.root.xml` (for example `fc3_main\__UNKNOWN\misc\7215F999AEACCD55.root.xml`, `multicommon\__UNKNOWN\misc\7FFFAC13D814F0C8.root.xml`) list `<CDominoBoxResource ID="Domino/...lua">`, textures, materials and more. Hashing these IDs names every file. Not done yet.
- **The `GetLocalPlayer` box is trivial:** `function GetLocalPlayer:In() self.LocalPlayer = GetLocalPlayerId(); Out(self) end`. So the single-player graphs reach the local player through the Lua global `GetLocalPlayerId`, and an override box file in our own archive could change it for 1484 call sites in `fc3_main`.
- **`FC3.dll` (x86, Ghidra 12.1.4, via REA):** string `GetLocalPlayerId` at `0x11fbdcc4` has two references. (1) `0x111ce912` inside `FUN_111cdb00`, the registration table of **console commands** (`GetLocalPlayerId` -> `FUN_111cb980`, which only formats a string, so it is not the Lua function; the same table has `TeleportToCurrentObjective`, `SetHealth`, `HitMe`, `GetCurrentPrimaryBuddy`, `SetNoWeaponMode`, `ActivateChallenge`, `CompleteChallenge`). (2) `0x11b1b5c4` inside `FUN_11b1b4e0` (4442 bytes), probably the registration of the **Lua global**. The string `Game:GetLocalPlayerId(%%)` at `0x11fba634` is referenced from `0x111ef365` (a very large function). Not decompiled yet.
- Not found in `FC3.dll` as strings: `PlayerCount`/`GetPlayerFromSlot` (only `m_playerCount: %i` at `0x11fafae8` and `MultiCaptureBarPlayerCount` at `0x12026c84`) and `Domino/System/Player`. Box names are not in the DLL; `PlayerCount` is a Lua box in `multicommon`.
- REA with `FC3.dll` (40 MB): first analysis takes about 10 minutes; the default start limit of 330 s fails. User-scope entry `rea` now has `REA_GHIDRA_STARTUP_TIMEOUT_MS=3600000`. Working copy: `Work\fc3\bin\FC3.dll` (SHA-256 equals the original). A duplicate project-scope `rea` entry (`npx -y`) caused the CONNECT_TIMEOUT and was removed.
- Next: decompile `FUN_11b1b4e0` and find the native function behind the Lua global `GetLocalPlayerId`; read the `PlayerCount` and `Coop/AllPlayers/*` box files; hash the manifest IDs to name all files; then decide A or B. (Done below.)

### Phase 1 findings: file names and the native co-op primitives (2026-10-24)

- **File names.** All 22 `*.root.xml` manifests hold 65217 unique resource IDs; hashing them (see the rule above) names 45045 of the 162363 hashed files (3555 are Lua; the other types are .mab, .xbt, .xbg, .spk, .bin, .dpax and so on). The list is `Work\fc3\names.tsv` (`hash`, `name`, `archives`). Text files (.lua, .xml) with real paths: `Work\fc3\named\<archive>\...` (4104 files). Not in any manifest, found by hashing a known path: the Lua libraries in `common` (for example `Domino/System/Coop/LuaLibraries/LuaLibCoop.lua` = `common\__UNKNOWN\unknown\D2329431C5AD239C`, `Domino/System/LuaLibraries/LuaLibStateless.lua` = `6FD38B2074BAB4CB`).
- **The engine already has 4 player slots in the single-player build.** Box `Domino/System/Player/GetPlayerFromSlot` is in `fc3_main` and is byte-identical to the one in `multicommon`. `LuaLibCoop:GetNrOfPlayers()` calls the native `GetNumberOfPlayers()` and maps 0 or 1 to 2; `SetNrOfPlayersOverride` accepts 2..4. Boxes `GetPlayers` register `OnSpawnPlayer_0..3` through `CScriptCallbackSystem_GetInstance():RegisterGenericCallback`. `AllPlayers/TeleportPlayer` calls the native `CoopTeleportPlayer(player, destination)`.
- **Lua entity ids are decimal strings** (`sprintf("%llu")` of a 64-bit id); the invalid id comes from `GetInvalidEntityId()`.
- **Native Lua functions in `FC3.dll`** (registered in the table around `0x11b1d252`..`0x11b1df2e`; the callback pushed just before the `mov edi, <name string>` belongs to that name; names at `0x1206ad0c`: `SpawnCoopAI`, `GetSlotForPlayer`, `GetPlayerInSlot`, `CoopGetCharacterName`, `GetNumberOfPlayers`, `CoopDoGetPawnState`):
  - `GetLocalPlayerId()` = `FUN_1065ef50` -> `FUN_10c79cb0`: reads the local-player provider `DAT_1280437c` (+8 holds an object whose vtable slot +0x10 returns a pointer to the 64-bit id), `-1` if there is none.
  - `GetPlayerInSlot(slot)` = `FUN_10680ef0` -> `FUN_10c73340(out, slot)`: global session pointer `DAT_12804acc`; if byte `+0x6c` is set, session object `S = FUN_1039e1f0(*(DAT_12804acc+0x68))`; slot table = `S+0xb8` (array of 32-bit handles, `-1` = empty), size at `S+0xbc`; a handle is resolved by `FUN_10260770` to a player object `P`, and the entity id is `**(P+0xcc)`. If slot 0 is empty, it falls back to `FUN_11305eb0()` (probably the single-player local player). Other empty slots give `-1`.
  - `GetNumberOfPlayers()` = `FUN_10681070`: `*(S+0x54)`, or 0 without a session.
  - `CoopGetCharacterName(entity)` = `FUN_10680f90`; `GetSlotForPlayer` = `FUN_10680ef0`'s neighbour (not checked); `CoopTeleportPlayer` registration xref `0x11b1d252` (callback not decoded yet).
- **Consequence for the A/B choice.** The single-player game already runs with a session object that has a 4-slot player table. If a second `P` (a puppet pawn) is put into slot 1, then `GetPlayerInSlot(1)`, `GetNumberOfPlayers()` and all the co-op boxes will see it. Open: what creates `S` and fills the slots in the single-player build, whether `S+0x54` is 1 there, and how a remote pawn would be spawned (`SpawnCoopAI`, `OnSpawnPlayer`). 
- Pairing rule check: `GetNumberOfPlayers` -> `FUN_10681070` (no argument, returns a count) and `GetPlayerInSlot` -> `FUN_10680ef0` (slot to id string) both match the Lua boxes; `FUN_10680f90` (entity to string) matches `CoopGetCharacterName`.
- Next: decompile `FUN_11305eb0`, `FUN_10260770`, `FUN_1039e1f0` (who creates the session object and the slot table); find the code behind `SpawnCoopAI` and `OnSpawnPlayer_%u` (`0x11fc1c64`); check `CoopTeleportPlayer`; then run a small test: an ASI that writes a fake handle into slot 1 and reads `GetNumberOfPlayers` from Lua. (Partly done below.)

### Phase 1 findings: the player service is a network object (2026-10-24)

- `S` is the component **`CFCXPlayerService`**. `FUN_1039e1f0(container)` walks a component array (`container+8`, count `container+0xc`) and returns the entry whose type (vtable slot +4) matches the static type from `FUN_1039e0d0` (`&DAT_12860b80`, name `CFCXPlayerService`); the last index is cached in `DAT_1217a7d4`. The container is `*(DAT_12804acc+0x68)`; `DAT_12804acc` has 4529 references (a central game/services object).
- The class is **network-replicated**: a static initializer at `0x11b6ff80` registers `NetObject.FCXPlayerService` (descriptor `0x12838424`) and net messages `PlayerService_HidePlayerMessage` / `PlayerService_ShowPlayerMessage` (`0x1283842c`, `0x12838434`). Other strings: `IPlayerService`, `CPlayerService`, `NetDiscoveryGroup.PlayerService`. Net registration code: `FUN_11839b60`, `FUN_10468480`.
- Slot-0 fallback `FUN_11305eb0` uses the same local-player provider as `GetLocalPlayerId` (`DAT_1280437c`), otherwise a static invalid id. So in single player slot 0 is the local player even with an empty slot table.
- **scubrah's Open World Co-op (secondary sources; the Nexus pages return HTTP 403 without login):** online co-op on the single-player map with enemy AI, vehicles, outposts and the full map, max players raised to 8, no story missions, about 2 GB download, alpha (2020-07-10). Sources: https://www.dsogaming.com/news/there-is-now-a-co-op-mod-for-far-cry-3-that-you-can-download/ , https://vgtimes.com/games/far-cry-3/files/24390-co-op-mode.html . The size suggests a repacked world archive (the island as a co-op map), that is, the engine's own co-op network session running on the island.
- **Working conclusion (not yet a decision):** transport **A** is likely viable: the engine's co-op session already works on the island (scubrah), and the player service, slots and co-op Lua API exist in the same DLL. The missing part is the story: single-player Domino graphs use `GetLocalPlayer` and know nothing about other players. Open risk from PLAN.md stays: how co-op sessions are found and joined today (Ubisoft matchmaking may be required).
- Next: the owner downloads mod 220 from Nexus (login needed) into `Downloads\fc3-open-world-coop\` so its archives can be unpacked and compared with the vanilla ones; find how a co-op session picks its world (strings `multicommon`, `fc3_main`, co-op map list); find the join path (direct IP or LAN versus Ubisoft services). (Join path: see below.)

### Phase 1 findings: network transport and join path (2026-10-24)

- **Network library: Ubisoft Storm** (`storm::echo::session`, `storm::echo::player`, `storm::echo::lan`, `storm::punch`). Sessions have host migration, tickets, join requests, peer routing through the host, and NAT traversal (`punchclient`, `punchtraversal`, `Storm NAT Client`).
- **Online services: Ubisoft RendezVous** (`CGameMatchmakingProvider_RdV`, `CRendezVousInitPunchOperation`, `onlineconfigservice.ubi.com`, `/OnlineConfigService.svc/GetOnlineConfig?onlineConfigID=`). The game menus use this path. Whether it still works today is unknown; a quick in-game check is to open the co-op menu and create a private match.
- **LAN exists in the engine:** `storm::echo::lan::inEvent::DiscoverSession`, `BroadcastLanDiscovery2`, `lan::outEvent::DiscoveredSession`, `lan::inFunc::GenerateSessionId`; Storm tunables `LanMatchmakingReuseAddress` ("SO_REUSEADDR to support many applications hosting sessions on the same machine"), `LanMatchmakingDiscoveryOnLoopbackEnable` ("discover sessions on the local machine only"), `LanMaxUploadInbps` / `LanInitialUploadInbps` / `LanMinUploadInbps`, also `DisableRouting`, `ForceRouting`, `SessionJoinTimeoutInMs` and other timeouts (strings around `0x11fb6f00`). The options menu has "LAN Match Making Port" (`OPTIONS_NETWORK_LANMATCHMAKINGPORT`), but no LAN game mode was found in the English UI strings. How the game switches to LAN is not found yet.
- **Engine config files:** the engine loads pairs `engine\settings\Default<X>Config.xml` and `engine\settings\Override<X>Config.xml` for X = Engine, Network, EngineServices, Threading, Render, Sound, Game, Realtree (+ `ProfileSpecific*GameConfig.xml`, `*engineconfig_legacy.xml`). The defaults exist in `common` (by hash): `DefaultNetworkConfig.xml` = `common\__UNKNOWN\misc\02E1D1848EC84585.Profile.xml`, `DefaultEngineConfig.xml` = `4C5BBCC4AC9D8E00`, `DefaultGameConfig.xml` = `50C61851314582D5`, `DefaultEngineServicesConfig.xml` = `5FDA7AF3F9DB5B99`. **No `Override*` file exists in the game**, so our own archive can add `OverrideNetworkConfig.xml` without replacing a game file.
- `DefaultNetworkConfig.xml`: `EnableNatTraversal=1`, `SecureSocketEnabled=1`, `MainSocketEncrypted=1`, `MaximumPeerCount=25`, `MigrationEnabled=1`, `OnlineEnginePort=9000`, `OnlineServicePort=9001`, `FileTransferHostPort=9002`, `FileTransferClientPort=9003`, `LanHostBroadcastPort=9004`, `LanClientBroadcastPort=9005`, `ScanFreePorts=1` from 9000 over 1000 ports; network log filters (including `session`, `lanmanager`), and **network emulator profiles** `moderate` / `high` (packet drop, delay, reorder) enabled by `EmulatorEnabled` + `EmulatorProfile`. Useful for lag tests on one PC.
- **Join parameters:** `FUN_11342740` registers reflected fields of a session-operation parameter struct: `SearchIdAddress` (+0x8), `SearchSessionId` (+0x10), `JoinSessionId` (+0x18), `SessionRefId` (+0x20), `DeleteSessionId` (+0x28), `PlaylistId` (+0x34), `SessionType` (+0x38), `NetEngineType` (+0x3c), `AccountType` (+0x40), `SearchRetries` (+0x44), `SearchSlots` (+0x48). Nearby strings: `JoinGame`, `JoinService`, `MatchMakingEnabled`, `fullNetworkInitialization`, `isInEditor`, `execLines`, `mapIdLow` / `mapIdHigh`, `saveGameFilename`. Net engine states: `ENetEngineState_Initialized_Offline` / `_Online`. Operations: `CFCXCreateSessionOperation`, `CFCXJoinSessionOperation`, `CFCXNetEngineStartupOperation`, `CSessionNetEnginePlayerAddOperation`.
- Command-line flag seen: `-enablenetobjectstats`.
- **What this means for the plan.** Transport A has two candidate join paths: (1) Storm LAN discovery, made to work over the internet the same way as in the Far Cry (2004) mod (a localhost relay plus Steam invite); (2) the Ubisoft online path, if its services still work. Both need a runtime check. Unknown: what selects LAN (`NetEngineType` value, a config option or a code path).
- Next (static): find the code that reads `NetEngineType` (offset +0x3c) and the creator of the LAN manager (`lan::inEvent::SetDiscoveryHandler`); (runtime, owner's PC): check whether the stock co-op menu still reaches Ubisoft services.
- **Update 2026-10-24 (owner):** Ubisoft online services for Far Cry 3 are shut down. The RendezVous path is gone; transport A must use Storm LAN (bridged over the internet by our relay) or we fall back to B. The references to `LanMatchmaking*` and the `lan` event types at `0x1070de..` are only type registration tables; the code that starts LAN discovery is not found yet.

### Tools: archive packer and an unpacker fix (2026-10-24)

- **Archive list the engine loads** (`0x120701c4`): `patch.dat`, `patch_%lang%.dat`, `patch_sound_%lang%.dat`, `common.dat`, `sound.dat`, `sound_%lang%.dat`, `ige.dat`, `igepatch.dat`, `shadersobj.dat`, `worlds/fc3_main/fc3_main{,_%lang%,_vistas}.dat`, `worlds/multicommon/multicommon{,_%lang%,_vistas}.dat`; also strings `_2.dat`, `subfat_%s.fat`, `mergedBigFileName` (meaning unknown). So a mod ships a rebuilt `patch.dat/fat` = the vanilla patch content plus our files (this is also how the owner's skip-cutscenes mod worked).
- **FAT v9 layout (checked on the shipped `patch.fat` and `common.fat`):** 24-byte header `FAT2`, version 9, flags `0x301` (platform 1 = Windows, compression version 3), `u0C = u10 = 0`, entry count; 20-byte entries sorted by hash; then 8 zero bytes. Uncompressed entries have scheme 0, `UncompressedSize = 0` and the size in `CompressedSize`. Data offsets are not aligned in shipped files.
- **Packer:** new project `Gibbed.FarCry3.Pack` (`bin_fc3\Gibbed.FarCry3.Pack.exe output.fat input_dir`, stores files uncompressed). Changes in Gibbed: `BigFileV2.Serialize` implemented for v9 only; `Pack` takes default version and compression version (9, 3 for FC3); files under `__UNKNOWN` now keep 16-digit hashes (the old code cut them to 8 digits, which only fits 32-bit hashes). LZO compression is still not implemented upstream (not needed).
- **Unpacker bug, fixed:** the unpacker read uncompressed entries with `UncompressedSize`, which is 0 in FC3, so every uncompressed file was written **empty** into `__UNKNOWN\null\`. Affected in the Phase 1 output: `patch` 4 (two `.bik` videos of 136 MB and 63 MB, two materials), `common` 429, `multicommon` 17108, `fc3_main` 11799 files, and 16 files in `named\`. Fixed in `EntryDecompression.cs` and the type guess in `Unpack.cs`; all four archives were unpacked again with the fix: `patch` 427, `common` 3099, `multicommon` 122255, `fc3_main` 64354 files, no empty files; `names.tsv` and `named\` rebuilt (45045 names, 0 empty text files). Helper scripts (name hashing, FAT dump, re-unpack): `scripts\`.
- **Round-trip check:** vanilla `patch.fat` unpacked, packed again and unpacked again: 427 of 427 files identical (MD5).
- Build needs the local NuGet cache (see below for the scubrah mod and version 1.05): `$env:NUGET_PACKAGES='E:\FarCryCoop\Data\nuget'`, `dotnet build ... --source E:\FarCryCoop\Data\nuget` (`env.ps1` does not set it). Patch file: [docs/gibbed-dunia-fc3.patch](docs/gibbed-dunia-fc3.patch).

### scubrah's Open World Co-op, patch 1.5 (2026-10-24)

- Downloaded by the owner into `Downloads\fc3-open-world-coop\` (from Nexus mod 220). Readme: install over the game; "create a private co-op session from the main menu and change the chapter to ROOK ISLANDS / fc3_main"; single player and stock co-op maps do not work while installed. The private session uses Ubisoft online services, which are shut down (owner, 2026-10-24), so the mod cannot start as shipped.
- **Its DLLs are a different engine build:** `FC3.dll` 29994856 bytes, PE timestamp 2018-05-02, PDB `E:\Work\FC3_Chinese\sub-pc\bin\FC3.pdb` (ours: 2022-10-03, `F:\Projects\FC3\bin\FC3.pdb`, 40720424 bytes). The `No_Minimap` variant differs in 6 data bytes (`A`/`H`/`R` -> `X` at file offsets `0x159c8b4`..`0x15cfbf6`). The mod's other DLL edits can only be found exactly by diffing against a clean DLL of the same build.
- **Versions:** the current Steam build (1.06, 2022) has no multiplayer; 1.05 is the last version with it (community guides). Depot 220241 manifest for 1.05 according to a Steam guide: `7362101836779063707` (not yet verified). Nexus mod 262 "Solo Offline Coop Game Mode" (based on 1.05) starts the co-op mode offline and is a reference for starting a session without servers.
- **Archives:** mod `patch.fat` has 3316 entries (vanilla 427): 2864 world sectors `.fcb` (`WorldSector` / `MissionLayer` / `Entity`), 404 Lua (main missions 150, `fc3_master` 91, DLC 66, `lib_*`), configs. Mod `fc3_main_vistas` has 129894 entries, 3 GB (vanilla 13261, 150 MB); about 100000 share hashes with `multicommon` and 25000 with `fc3_main`, 14309 are new. Unpacked to `Work\fc3\mod\`; vanilla vistas to `Work\fc3\fc3_main_vistas\`. Full content comparison: `Work\fc3\mod\compare.tsv` (script `scripts\contentcompare.py`).
- **Configs in the mod:** `DefaultNetworkConfig.xml` turns on the network emulator with a new profile `upstream32k` whose `upload_bandwidth bits_per_sec="0"` (no drop, no delay), sets `ObjectTrafficStatsEnabled=1` and doubles the upload limits to 20480000. Two engine `Profile.xml` files raise streaming rings (`RingData="15,16,10,20,1"`, `RingLod="10,12"`) and physics limits. The author used FCBConverter (ArmanIII) for `.fcb` <-> XML.
- **Plan:** download clean 1.05 `bin` and `patch` with DepotDownloader 3.4.0 (`Tools\depotdownloader-3.4.0`, zip SHA-256 `41C9E9F0DF54B3AD02E67A11726756E5C73283BD7C2E1B04ACFA5AE4C2ED3767`, size checked against the GitHub release; the exe is not signed) into `Downloads\fc3-1.05\`, file list `scripts\fc3-105-filelist.txt`; the owner runs it with his Steam login. Then diff the clean 1.05 DLL against the mod DLL to get the exact mod patches.

### Clean 1.05 and the exact DLL edits of the mod (2026-10-24)

- Downloaded by the owner with DepotDownloader (`-qr` login) into `Downloads\fc3-1.05\`: `bin\` and `data_win32\patch*`, `igepatch*`. `FC3.dll` 1.05: 29994856 bytes, SHA-256 `98e34155266de3627202e520bff92b2be17a21ee3f89d9a6213915b83e9e1166`, PE timestamp 2018-05-02, PDB GUID `b0ba4c20...` = the same build as the mod's DLL. So manifest `7362101836779063707` is 1.05 and is the base of scubrah's mod. 1.05 `bin` still has `uplay_r1_loader.dll` and `ubiorbitapi_r2_loader.dll`.
- **Owner decision:** support both 1.05 and 1.06 (PLAN.md).
- **scubrah's DLL edits = 90 bytes in 45 runs**, the same in `FC3.dll` and `FC3_d3d11.dll` (script `scripts\patchctx.py`; offsets for `FC3.dll` 1.05):
  - `.rdata` `0x11535dd0`: string `mpdlc1_aegir` -> `fc3_main`. A multiplayer DLC map name is replaced by the single-player world, which is how the island shows up as a co-op chapter.
  - world bounds for network quantization: block at `0x11535bb0` and single floats: +/-1536 -> +/-5120, +/-1029 -> +/-5120, 1535.99/2047.99/1279.99 -> 5119.99, 127.99/255.99 -> 511.99/512, 129 -> 512, 153600 -> 512000, 12900 -> 51200, 1.536e6 -> 5.12e6. The co-op maps are about 3 km wide, the island about 10 km.
  - code: three serializer helpers `push 8` -> `push 9` (`0x1027b5da`, `0x1027b5fb`), `push 6` -> `push 7` (`0x1027b627`, `0x1027b648`), `push 7` -> `push 8` (`0x10592f7a`, `0x10592f9b`): one more bit each in pairs (write and read), matching the larger coordinate range.
  - names renamed to switch features off (`F`/`C` -> `X`): FOV-shift camera properties (`fBlendOutFOVTime` and others), `CRadioTowerPanelComponent`, `CoopNameplatesUI` / `CFCXCoopNameplatesUI` (readme: "Removed NPC tag icon"). The `No_Minimap` variant adds 6 more bytes of the same kind.
- **Portability to 1.06:** the data anchors exist in the 1.06 DLL (`mpdlc1_aegir` at file `0x1f893b4`, bounds block at `0x207b8e0`, `CoopNameplatesUI`, `fBlendOutFOVTime`), but the three serializer code patterns do not match byte for byte; they need function matching.
- **Mod archives by content** (`Work\fc3\mod\compare.tsv`, compared with the 1.06 data): `fc3_main_vistas`: 115585 files byte-identical to vanilla (`multicommon`, `fc3_main`, vistas) plus 14309 new; `patch`: 396 identical, 64 changed, 2856 new (2827 `.fcb` world sectors). Whether the new files are vanilla files under new names is being checked (`dupcheck.py`).
- **Address map script** `scripts\fc3_addrmap.py` (no Ghidra, seconds): finds anchor strings, code references, the registered callback and the globals the callback reads. Handles both registration layouts: 1.06 `push 0; push CB; call; mov edi, name` (callback before the name) and 1.05 `push name; push esi; push edi; call; push ebx; push CB; push edi; call` (callback after the name). Self-check against the hand-verified 1.06 values (`--selftest`): passes. Output: `Work\fc3\addrmap-105.tsv`, `addrmap-106.tsv`.
- 1.05 callbacks, **confirmed by decompiling** (REA snapshot `E:\FarCryCoop\Data\rea-snapshots\fc3-105-FC3.dll.snapshot`): `GetPlayerInSlot` `0x10bad843` (int slot -> 64-bit id), `GetNumberOfPlayers` `0x10baba48` (no argument -> number, via `FUN_10bca625`), `CoopGetCharacterName` `0x10badb62` (`EntityId` argument), `CoopDoGetPawnState` `0x10bad8b5` (int argument -> number); from the script, not yet decompiled: `GetLocalPlayerId` `0x10bb3f60`, `GetSlotForPlayer` `0x10bad7cb`, `CoopTeleportPlayer` `0x10bac7d0`, `SpawnCoopAI` `0x10bae756`, `GetInvalidEntityId` `0x10bb7709`. Both builds use the same layout: the callback is pushed just before the name (an earlier "callback after the name" rule for 1.05 was wrong and is removed from the script; `--selftest` now checks both builds).

### Nexus mod 262 "Solo Offline Coop Game Mode" (2026-10-24)

- Downloaded by the owner into `Downloads\fc3-solo-offline-coop\`: `FC3.dll`, `FC3_d3d11.dll`, `patch.dat/fat` (dated 2022-07-20), built on 1.05.
- **DLL: only two edits**, both replace `call 0x108c4b09` with five NOPs (1.05 `FC3.dll` at `0x108c4c6b` and `0x108c5c36`; `FC3_d3d11.dll` file offsets `0xa1e1e2`, `0xa1f1ad`). Context: `74 07 8b ce e8 ..` and `80 7d ff 00 74 07 8b cb e8 ..` (a call made only when a flag is set). Hypothesis: the check that leaves the session when the online services are missing. To confirm by decompiling (REA analysis of 1.05 running).
- **patch: vanilla 1.05 patch (427 files, unchanged) + 4 files:** `common` overrides `9B788D77FCFA35BB.fcb` (game database, 1040772 -> 2617955 bytes; only 12 new strings, so mostly numeric/structural changes) and `E88B7431DC8E7DBF.feu` (main menu UI in a Ubisoft `UEF` container, +3 KB); `36A67CE0D3C22326.xml` (input action map, the same file scubrah ships); one Lua `coop_vidar_defend` "AllInRoom" with the door-closing sequence commented out.
- Next: decompile `0x108c4b09` and its two callers in 1.05; unpack the `UEF` menu (JPEXS or own parser) to see the menu change; convert the `.fcb` with FCBConverter to see the database change.
- **FCBConverter** (Jakub Marecek, alias ArmanIII; source https://github.com/JakubMarecek/FCBConverter, build 20230711-1830 from https://downloads.fcmodding.com/files/FCBConverter.zip, no published hash; zip SHA-256 `803B04CC9DA0D40BF2CB390C1D1C0EB26D6AFFDAD1E6D4E0360EED8E63B3BDCE`) in `Tools\fcbconverter-20230711\FCBConverter\`. Supports FC3 (FAT v9). It needs .NET 7; it runs on our portable .NET 8 with `DOTNET_ROOT=E:\FarCryCoop\Tools\dotnet-sdk-8.0.426` and `DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD=Major` (no install on C:). Usage: `FCBConverter.exe -source=<file.fcb>` writes `<file>.converted.xml`. It ships `FCBConverterFileNames.list` (4189677 file names) and `FCBConverterStrings.list` (483419 strings) that can name more of our hashes.
- **The mod 262 database change** (`9B788D77FCFA35BB.fcb`, converted in `Work\fc3-105\fcb\`): only two fields in the playlist **"Local Coop"** (`id` 0xFFFFFFFC, `sessionType` 3, `maxPlayersInRound` 2): `minPlayersInRound` 2 -> 1 and `matchCountdownInSecs` 0 -> 10. The binary is larger only because of how the converter writes it. Playlists in the vanilla database: `sessionType` 2 = User (12), Beta Test (14), Map Archive (14), Private Coop (4); `sessionType` 3 = Local Coop (2). So mod 262 starts the stock "Local Coop" session type (probably the console split-screen mode) with one player, without servers. This is offline, not network play, but it shows that a co-op world can start without Ubisoft services.
- **What the new files of scubrah's mod are** (`dupcheck.py`: only 6 of 17165 "new" files are vanilla files under another name): the 2827 new `.fcb` in `patch` are world sectors with the author's own entities `CoopAI_EncounterPawn_<random>` (co-op AI encounter pawns, with cover points) and `CoopVehicle_<random>` (vehicles with destroyed variants); the random suffixes mean they were generated by a tool. So the island is populated with co-op AI spawners and vehicles instead of the single-player scripts. The 14309 new vistas files start with `04 4B 50 53` (probably `.spk` sound packages). Samples: `Work\fc3\mod\sample\`.
- **More file names:** `scripts\names_ext.py` hashes the 4189677 paths of `FCBConverterFileNames.list`: +84934 names (of 192788 hashed files across vanilla and mod archives, 62809 still unnamed). Result `Work\fc3\names_ext.tsv` (column `source` = manifest or fcbconverter); 1395 more Lua/XML copied into `named\`. Key names: `9B788D77FCFA35BB` = `generated\nomadobjecttemplates.fcb` (object templates incl. playlists), `E88B7431DC8E7DBF` = `ui\multiplayer\menu\multimain.feu` (multiplayer main menu, changed by mod 262), `8BDCD85D74264EB2` = `engine\settings\pc\defaultengineconfig.xml`, `36A67CE0D3C22326` = `actionmaps\inputactionmapcommon.xml`; scubrah's sectors are `worlds\fc3_main\levels\<cell>\generated\worldsectors\worldsector_<n>.data.fcb` (2834 files, absent from the vanilla archives).
- **Mod 262's DLL edits disable "session ended: not enough players" (decompiled, 1.05):** `FUN_108c4b09` clears `[this+0x180]`, builds a 0xE4-byte message box (vtable `0x1156d990`) with the text from the loc-id table at `0x115ac030` = {167676, 167677, 167678} = `*_END_OF_GAME_INSUFFICIENT_PLAYERS` ("The game session ended prematurely because of an insufficient number of players.") and the title 160927 ("Alert"; its enum is `MP_LinkApp_Passport_Locked_Alert`, but the string is only reused as a generic title), then posts an event of type `DAT_118644bc` (ends the session). Callers in 1.05: `FUN_108c4c48` at `0x108c4c6b` (`if (event type == DAT_118644bc && [this+0x180]) ...`) and `0x108c5c36` (after a loop over the players, when a flag stays 0). These are exactly the two calls mod 262 replaces with NOPs. **Correction:** an earlier entry here called this a Uplay Passport check because of the title's enum name; that was wrong, it is not a licence check.
- **The same function in 1.06:** the loc-id table is at `0x11ffac34`, referenced from `0x10ff276f`, function start `0x10ff2750`; three callers: `0x10fddbc8` and `0x10ff3be4` (`cmp eax,[0x1283a184]; jne; cmp byte [reg+0x180],0; je; push reg; call`, the event-type check) and `0x10ff2adc` (`cmp byte [esp+0x13],0; je; push edi; call`, after the player loop). Confirmed by decompiling 1.06: `FUN_10ff2750(param)` clears `[param+0x180]`, takes the text from `0x11ffac34`, title `0x2749F`, posts an event of type `DAT_1283a184`; the object is passed on the stack (hence `push reg` at the call sites). REA snapshots: `E:\FarCryCoop\Data\rea-snapshots\fc3-105-FC3.dll.snapshot` (38 KB) and `fc3-106-FC3.dll.snapshot` (11 KB); they are small, so they probably keep only evidence and annotations, not the Ghidra database; whether reopening with them skips the analysis is not checked yet.
- **Owner decision 2026-10-24: the "session ended: not enough players" end is disabled in our mod** (as in mod 262), so a session can run with one player. The owner approved it when it was still believed to be a Passport check; after decompiling it turned out to be a player-count rule, not a licence check. The game's own protection (launch through Ubisoft Connect with a licence on the account) stays untouched; every player needs an own copy.

### Prototype v0: solo co-op session without servers, rebuilt with our own tools (2026-10-24)

- **Test copies** (`scripts\build_testgame.ps1 -Version 1.05|1.06`): `E:\FarCryCoop\Work\game-105` and `game-106`. `bin\` is a real copy (1.05 from `Downloads\fc3-1.05`, 1.06 from the install); `data_win32\` holds 94 hard links to the install's archives (same volume, no extra space) and real copies of `patch*` / `igepatch*`. Never edit a hard-linked file in place; always replace it with a new file. `Support\` is not copied. Install checked afterwards: `FC3.dll` and `patch.fat` hashes unchanged. The vanilla `patch.fat` is identical in 1.05 and 1.06.
- **DLL patcher** `scripts\patch_dll.py`: finds the "not enough players" function through its loc-id table and NOPs every call to it; works on all four DLLs; refuses paths inside the Steam install; `--selftest`: the patched 1.05 `FC3.dll` and `FC3_d3d11.dll` equal mod 262's files byte for byte, a second run is a no-op. Applied to the test copies: 1.05 `FC3.dll` function `0x108c4b09` (calls `0x108c4c6b`, `0x108c5c36`), `FC3_d3d11.dll` `0x10a1ec80` (`0x10a1ede2`, `0x10a1fdad`); 1.06 `FC3.dll` `0x10ff2750` (`0x10fddbc8`, `0x10ff2adc`, `0x10ff3be4`), `FC3_d3d11.dll` `0x10ff7f90` (`0x10fe33f8`, `0x10ff831c`, `0x10ff9424`).
- **Database edit** `scripts\edit_playlist.py` on the FCBConverter XML of `generated\nomadobjecttemplates.fcb`: playlist "Local Coop" `minPlayersInRound=1`, `matchCountdownInSecs=10`; converted back with FCBConverter: byte-identical to mod 262's file (SHA-256 `9A3C6D8D...`).
- **Patch archive** `scripts\build_patch.ps1` -> `Work\mod-build\patch.dat/fat`: vanilla patch (427 files) + our `generated\nomadobjecttemplates.fcb` + **test only, third-party** `ui\multiplayer\menu\multimain.feu` from mod 262 (not ours, never shipped; to be replaced by our own menu change). Unpack check: 429 files. Installed into both test copies.
- Next: the owner starts `Work\game-105\bin\farcry3.exe` (then 1.06) and tries the multiplayer menu -> Local Coop with one player. Expected: the island-less co-op map loads alone without the "insufficient number of players" end. 1.05 test copy uses the 1.06 data archives (only `bin` and `patch` are 1.05); if it fails at load, download the full 1.05 depot.

### Prototype v0 tests on the owner's PC (2026-10-24)

- **1.06** (`Work\game-106`, our mod): starts; the "Multiplayer" button immediately shows "The online multiplayer service is not available anymore." Origin: `FUN_11751440` (loc string lookup, 1.06) returns a hard-coded UTF-16 text (English at `0x11f17378`, translations next to it, selected by `FUN_11750980`) when the loc id is `0x23894` = 145556 = `X360_EU_NETWORK_XLSP_DOWN` ("The Far Cry 3 service is not available. Please try again later."). So 1.06 still tries the Ubisoft online service when entering multiplayer; Ubisoft only changed the text. To reach a co-op session in 1.06 this online login path must be bypassed in code.
- **1.05 startup:** `farcry3.exe` (DX9) shows a black screen in fullscreen (with both `UseD3D11` 1 and 0 in the profile); `farcry3_d3d11.exe` works in fullscreen; `farcry3.exe` works in a window (`Fullscreen="0"`). The profile `C:\Users\<user>\Documents\My Games\Far Cry 3\GamerProfile.xml` is shared by 1.05 and 1.06. With the owner's permission it was changed for the tests: `UseD3D11="0"`, `Fullscreen="0"`; backup of the original: `E:\FarCryCoop\Work\fc3-profile\GamerProfile.xml.bak` (restore after testing). No log files are written there.
- **1.05 data:** the 1.05 depot manifest (`Downloads\fc3-1.05-manifest\`, 326 files, 2018-05-21) equals the 1.06 install except `bin\` and two extra 1.05 files `worlds\multicommon\multicommon_japanese.dat/fat` (downloaded, now in `game-105`; `build_testgame.ps1` copies them). The 8 `sound_*.dat` differences are a false positive (empty files; the manifest lists SHA-1 zero for them).
- **1.05 with the mod:** vanilla 1.05 shows only "online co-op"; with our patch (and with mod 262's exact `patch`) the Coop menu shows **"Offline coop"**, but starting it **crashes**: access violation in the same function in both builds: `FC3.dll` `FUN_10622c58`+0x27 (`0x10622c7f`, DX9) and `FC3_d3d11.dll` `0x1077e1bd`+0x27 (`0x1077e1e4`). The code loops over an array: `mov esi, <result of call>; cmp [esi], 0; jbe ...; mov eax, [esi+4]; mov eax, [eax+ebx*4]` -> count > 0 but data pointer bad. Not caused by our tools (mod 262's own patch crashes the same way), not by the Japanese archive, not by the renderer. Decompiling `FUN_10622c58` (REA analysis of 1.05 running).
- **Offline coop crash: cause and fix (2026-10-25).** `FUN_10622c58` (1.05) iterates the list returned by `FUN_10622144`, which calls `UPLAY_PARTY_GetFullMemberList(this+0xc0)`; today's Ubisoft Connect no longer serves the Uplay party API, the call fails (`UPLAY_GetLastError`), the list keeps garbage (count > 0, bad data pointer) and the loop crashes. `FUN_10622c58` has no direct callers (called through a table). Fix in `patch_dll.py`: the `jbe` that skips an empty list becomes `jmp` (one byte, `76 3C` -> `EB 3C`), found by pattern `call; mov esi,eax; xor ebx,ebx; cmp [esi],ebx; jbe +3c; mov eax,[esi+4]; mov eax,[eax+ebx*4]`: 1.05 `FC3.dll` `0x10622c7a`, `FC3_d3d11.dll` `0x1077e1df`; the pattern does not occur in 1.06 (new UPC API). Self-test: our 1.05 DLLs = mod 262 DLLs + exactly this byte.
- **Prototype v0 works on 1.05 (owner's test, 2026-10-25):** `Work\game-105` with our patch archive and patched DLLs: Coop menu -> "Offline coop" -> the co-op map loads with one player, no crash, no "insufficient number of players". The test profile on C: is still in windowed DX9 mode (backup in `Work\fc3-profile\`).

### Session layer on PC 1.05: no LAN provider (2026-10-25)

- Account flags (`FUN_110d39c8`): `Offline` 1, `LAN` 2, `Live` 4, `Gator` 0x20, `Rendezvous` 0x40. Login operation fields (`FUN_104c0f03`): `login` (+4), `password` (+8), `onlineKey` (+0xc), `guestlogin` (+0x10), `primaryAccount` (+0x14), `accountFlags` (+0x18), `controllerIndex` (+0x1c), `silentLogin` (+0x1d), `isPrimaryIndex` (+0x1e), `platformSpecificId` (+0x20).
- The network config (`FUN_1024dbef`, profile `NetworkProfile` / `engine\settings\*NetworkConfig.xml`) has a list `Accounts` of `Account` (`CAccountConfig`, offset 0x6c) and a string `SessionProvider` (0x64). Test with the owner's permission: `<Accounts><Account login="Host" accountFlags="2" .../></Accounts>` added to `GamerProfile.xml` (backup `Work\fc3-profile\GamerProfile.xml.before-lan`): the game starts, the menus do not change.
- Reason: every game-side online provider in the PC build exists only as `_RdV` (Ubisoft RendezVous), `_UplayPC` or `_Win`: `CSessionLoginOperationProvider_RdV`, `CGameMatchmakingProvider_RdV`, `CGameInviteProvider_RdV`, `CGameFriendProvider_RdV` / `_UPlayPC`, `CGamePartyProvider_UplayPC`, `CGameFrontendServiceProvider_RdV` / `_Win` and more. There is no LAN provider; Storm's `storm::echo::lan` exists below but the PC game layer never uses it.
- Other findings: `-offline` command-line switch (`FUN_10680f27` picks a message mode; `FUN_10922ac6`, a debug main menu with "COOP Story" / "Adversarial" / "UPlay" / "!What Sebra did before this ;)!", hides the online entries), `-splitscreen`, `-splitscreen4`, `CFCXSplitScreenService`, `CGRQueryIsLocalCoopGame`, `OfflinePlaylist`.
- **Consequence:** with the Ubisoft services gone, a networked co-op session on PC needs one of: (A1) our own replacements for the `_RdV` login / matchmaking / invite providers in the ASI (answer locally and create or join a Storm session by address); (A2) a local emulator of the RendezVous service (redirect the service hosts to it); (B) our own netcode on top of the working local "Offline coop" session. To decide next.

### Developers' quick-launch: direct host / client without matchmaking (2026-10-25)

- `FUN_10652061` (1.05, one caller `0x10652215`) checks the command line: `-host` -> `FUN_106518ba`, `-client` -> `FUN_10651d10`, `-wait`, `-quickmatch`.
- **Host** (`FUN_106518ba`): needs `-login <name>`; reads `-host <value>` (map), `-bfname`, `-dedicated`, `-sessionuid <u64>`, game mode switch `-deathmatch` / `-coop` / `-domination` / `-headquarters` / `-firestorm` / `-dash`, `-class`, `-spawn`, `-spawnpos x,y,z`, `-spawnangle`, `-netemulator <profile>`, `-ranked`. Session type = **3 without `-online`** (the same local type as the "Local Coop" playlist), 2 with `-online`.
- **Client** (`FUN_10651d10`): needs `-login <name>` and `-ip <address>` ("Missing -login or -ip for -client."); `-online` additionally needs `-password`; `localhost` has its own branch; same mode switches, `-sessionuid`, `-class`, `-ranked`.
- Other switches found: `-xpos -ypos -xres -yres` (window), `-numclients`, `-offline`, `-quickstartup`, `-skipintro`, `-world`, `-exec`, `-execline`, `-login2..4`, `-password2..4`, `-splitscreen`, `-splitscreen4`, `-netemulator`, `-cmdport`, `-logFile`, `-steam_mode`, `-fromuplay`.
- Co-op map names: `coop_vidar`, `coop_skadi`, `coop_freyja`, `coop_huginn`, `coop_sleipnir`, `coop_fenrir`; also `fc3_main`, `mpdlc1_aegir`, `mpdlc1_idun`.
- Session operations available: `CSessionCreateOperation`, `CSessionSearchIPOperation`, `CSessionJoinOperation`, `CSessionStartNetOperation`, `CFCXCreateSessionOperation`, `CFCXJoinSessionOperation` and others. The login provider `CSessionLoginOperationProvider_RdV` (type getter `0x110cdef8`, vtable around `0x11696d98`, methods `0x110cd961`, `0x110cd71d`, `0x110ccb46`, `0x110cc8ac`) is a RendezVous job state machine.
- Test script: `scripts\launch_105.ps1 -Role host|client [-Map coop_vidar] [-Ip 127.0.0.1] [-Dx9]` (two windowed instances side by side; DX11 `farcry3_d3d11.exe` by default).

### Quick-launch test: host + client on one PC (2026-10-25)

- **Single-instance check.** A second copy stops with "You already have a Far Cry 3 session (game or editor) active or left open after an irregular shutdown" (`ERROR_INSTANCE_MUTEX`). Start-up code: `push 0x1f0001; call [OpenMutexA]; test eax,eax; jnz <error>`. `patch_dll.py` turns the `jnz` into 6 NOPs: 1.05 `FC3.dll` `0x100041bb`, `FC3_d3d11.dll` `0x10004092`. Two copies now run side by side.
- **Network address.** The host binds UDP 9000 on the first adapter, here the VirtualBox host-only adapter `192.168.56.1`, not `127.0.0.1`; the client binds 9001. The client was started with `-ip 192.168.56.1`.
- **Result:** the host loads `coop_vidar` (the world is visible behind the menu); the client appears to join and shows the same screen. Both then show an empty dark panel without text or buttons; the picture stops updating (mouse cursor trails), input does nothing. After pressing an "OK" button on the host it looks the same. Same with DX9 and DX11, with and without RivaTuner (RTSS) overlay.
- **Not a hang:** both processes respond to Windows; the main thread uses one core and keeps running different game code and driver calls (`scripts\thread_eips.ps1` samples thread EIPs and scans the stack for `FC3*.dll` addresses).
- **Suspects / next:** (1) the test-only third-party `multimain.feu` from mod 262 in our `patch.dat` breaks the network menu UI: retest with the patched DLL and the vanilla `patch.dat`; (2) a lobby screen (character select / ready) that waits for something from the missing online services; (3) analyse `FC3_d3d11.dll` in REA and map the main-thread stack addresses (e.g. `0x10338ad4`, `0x102f1b1d`, `0x103e07db`, `0x1040aaa3`, `0x10327bbd`).

## Session notes appended 2026-11-02 (agent session on the owner's PC)

Sections below were written in the agent session on the owner's PC. Their Phase 1 material
is not duplicated here: this file keeps the owner's granular Phase 1 sections as the
authoritative Phase 1 record. Paths in the appended sections are relative to this repo
(`scripts/` for analysis scripts, `local/` and `local/scratch/` for gitignored state).

## Phase 2 findings (2026-11-01, FC3.dll binary analysis)

**Network stack: Storm (Ubisoft)** — confirmed in FC3.dll strings:
- `storm echo` — session management (Create, ChangeSessionDescription, SessionTicket)
- `storm punch` — NAT traversal (ClientImplMainInitParams, ClientImplNetworkInitParams, detectNat, qos punch/traversal)
- `storm echo` — player events: RequestJoin, AcceptJoinRequest, RefuseJoinRequest, RequestAddPlayer, RemovePlayer, UpdatePlayer
- `AcceptSessionHostCandidacy` / `RefuseSessionHostCandidacy` — host election
- `BroadcastLanDiscovery2` — LAN discovery via UDP broadcast
- `Host disconnected starts host migration` — host migration supported
- `Listening on host port` — server listen
- `WS2_32.dll` — Winsock import confirmed
- `-dedicated` flag — dedicated server mode exists
- `dedicatedConfig`, `NumPlayers`, `SessionId`, `IsDedicated`, `MapSource`

**Co-op entities in engine:**
- `CSpawnPointCoop`, `CLocalSpawnPointCoopEntity` — co-op-specific spawn points
- `CSpawnPointMP`, `CSpawnPointBlue`, `CSpawnPointRed`, `CSpawnPointShared` — MP spawn types
- `CGRQueryIsCoopGame` — co-op game mode query
- `FCXCoop` — co-op game mode identifier

**Lobby/session infrastructure:**
- `CLobbyService`, `CNetGameCtrlEnterLobby`, `CNetGameCtrlStateUpdateLobby`
- `CSessionSendInviteOperation`, `CSessionAcceptInviteOperation`, `CSessionCancelInviteOperation`
- `CVotingService`, `CPlaylistService`
- `CFCXGRStateLobby` — co-op lobby state
- Connection states: `ResolvingAddress`, `Reaching`, `Connected`, `Dropped`, `Deleted`
- Error states: `CONNECTION_FAILED`, `CONNECTION_TIMED_OUT`, `KICKED`, `BANNED`, `SESSION_JOIN_TIMED_OUT`, `REFUSED`, `MIGRATION`

**Game modes in FC3.dll:**
- `Singleplayer.feu`, `Multiplayer` menu, `QuickLaunch.feu`
- `frontendworld`, `multicommon` — world references

**Conclusion:** The engine has a complete co-op infrastructure (Storm netcode, session management, LAN discovery, host migration, spawn points). The `-host`/`-client` flags and session type 3 (co-op story) should work. The "not enough players" check at `0x108c4b09` is the main gate to bypass for solo/offline play.

## Startup / command-line behaviour of the Uplay 1.06 build (2026-11-01)

Verified on the owner's machine: `H:/Uplay/Far Cry 3/bin` = Uplay build 1.06 (version number is NOT
readable from PE resources; build identity below is re-verified with
`scripts/fc3_version_probe.py` / `scripts/fc3_rsrc_probe.py` on 2026-11-01.

| file | bytes | sha256 (short) | PE TimeDateStamp |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `bin/FC3.dll` | 40720424 | `71d58a7a4503c5c6…` | 2022-10-03T12:21:31 |
| `bin/FC3_d3d11.dll` | 40869416 | `e541594e11ac6091…` | 2022-10-03T09:36:32 |
| `bin/farcry3_d3d11.exe` | 203816 | `5437b2e37a54f8a3…` | 2022-10-03T09:37:26 |
| `bin/farcry3.exe` | 203816 | `fd3929f19ff82591…` | 2022-10-03 |

`H:/Uplay/Far Cry 3/bin` and `H:/FarCryCoop/TestFC3/bin` are byte-identical (16 entries, no
`FC3_coop*` files).

**Correction (2026-11-01): the numbers in the earlier version of this section were wrong.** The old
text claimed PE compile time 2022-05-04, an exe size of 60992 bytes, file version 1.06.0.90512 and
product 1089053420, and MD5 `916c6d4b876d8be5f0ad68f50719a838` (FC3_d3d11) /
`5742de2aaa76935d46947bf89e2e2eb7` (FC3) / `47d4c992b0e82b7d52cc48e8d1bd9119` (farcry3_d3d11.exe).
The compile time is 2022-10-03, the exe is 203816 bytes, and **none of these binaries contain a
version resource** (`VS_VERSION_INFO` is absent; the only `FileVersion` hit is the
`GetFileVersionInfoA` import name) — so `1.06.0.90512` is not derivable from the PE files. The MD5
list may belong to a different (1.05) build; the sha256 values in the table above are what this
machine has. Bantila's recorded 1.05 `FC3.dll` SHA-256
`493031278308793D841F7419C83A447678651860312F56506A0A18B2A70D19E5` matches nothing here.
No 1.05 build exists anywhere on the disks (%USERPROFILE%/Downloads, Desktop, D:/, E:/, H:/).

- **The exe is a DRM stub.** `bin/farcry3_d3d11.exe` (203816 bytes; PE32, EP `0x000012de`,
  base `0x00400000`, `.text` 2138 B, `.rsrc` 187880 B, imports: `FC3_d3d11.dll` **1 function**,
  `MSVCR100.dll` 24, `KERNEL32.dll` 17 — no `GetCommandLine*`, no `CreateProcess*`, no `ShellExecute*`)
  starts, then re-executes the
  **canonical install** exe resolved from the registry
  (`HKLM\SOFTWARE\WOW6432Node\Ubisoft\Far Cry 3`: `InstallDir=H:/Uplay/Far Cry 3`,
  `ExecutableName=bin/FC3Updater.exe`, SKU=WW, Version=1.05, Language=Russian), passing only
  `-language=Russian`. Every extra argument is dropped.
- Consequence for the test copy: launching `H:/FarCryCoop/TestFC3/bin/farcry3_d3d11.exe` with
  `-host fc3_main -login h1 -coop` starts a child from `H:\Uplay\Far Cry 3\bin` with
  `-language=Russian` only. The test copy cannot run its own quick-launch.
- The quick-launch strings ARE present in 1.06 (`FC3.dll` command-line table `0x1fb6f6c`-
  `0x1fb74f6`): `-host` @0x1fb73cb, `-client` @0x1fb7472, `-login` @0x1fb7370, `-coop` @0x1fb73fc,
  `-offline` @0x1f36444, `-sessionuid` @0x1fb73e4, `-dedicated` @0x1f0d9dc,
  "Missing -login or -ip" @0x1fb7458.
  **Those offsets do not match the binary on this machine.** They look like raw-file offsets, but
  `0x1fb73cb` is outside the `.text` raw range of the installed `FC3.dll` (`.text` rawptr `0x400`,
  rawsize `0x1BC2000`; `.rdata` VA `0x11bcb000` rawptr `0x1bca200`; conversion
  `VA = 0x10001000 + (raw - 0x400)` for `.text`, `VA = 0x11bcb000 + (raw - 0x1bca200)` for `.rdata`).
  Verified string addresses on the installed 1.06 `FC3.dll` (`scripts/fc3_iat_scan.py`,
  `scripts/fc3_cmdline_cluster.py`): `-host` `0x11f0e6ac`, `-client` `0x11f0e748`, `-dedicated`
  `0x11f0e7dc`, `-login` `0x11fb8170`, `-sessionuid` `0x11fb81e4`, `-coop` `0x11fb81fc`, `-online`
  `0x11fb8234`, `-ip` `0x11fb8254`, `-bfname` `0x11fb7d64`, `-offline` `0x11f37244`.
  Installed `FC3_d3d11.dll`: `-host` `0x11f31c7c`, `-client` `0x11f31d18`, `-dedicated` `0x11f31dac`,
  `-login` `0x11fdc040`, `-sessionuid` `0x11fdc0b4`, `-coop` `0x11fdc0cc`, `-online` `0x11fdc104`,
  `-ip` `0x11fdc124`, `-bfname` `0x11fdbc34`, `-offline` `0x11f5a654`.
  What is **absent** in 1.06: the 1.05 debug-menu strings
  "Not enough players", "Local Coop", "Coop Story".
- **Resolved (2026-11-05, re-verified with `scripts/fc3_mode_cascade.py`): the mode-flag cascade.**
  The cascade is a chain of `push <flag string>` / `push esi` / `call edi` (`strstr`), `add esp,8`,
  `test eax,eax`, `je <next flag>`; a match reads a **static mode id** global into `[ebp+8]`.
- **Cascade body location (FC3.dll): `0x11a8c440`**
  (`push ebp` / `mov ebp,esp` / `sub esp,0x2c` / `push ebx` / `mov ebx,[ebp+0x14]` /
  `mov esi,[ebp+0xc]` / `push edi` / `mov edi,[0x11bcb5c0]` = `MSVCR100.dll:strstr`). The previously
  recorded range `0x11a8c533 .. 0x11a8c567` is a fragment of this body.
- **Flag order and match sites (FC3.dll, `0x11a8c440 .. 0x11a8c5b1`):** `-login` `0x11a8c455`,
  `-ip` `0x11a8c464`, `Missing -login or -ip for -client.` `0x11a8c476`, `-online` `0x11a8c49d`,
  `-password` `0x11a8c4ac`, `Trying to launch an online client, but missing password (-password)`
  `0x11a8c4be`, `-ip` `0x11a8c4f0`, `-bfname` `0x11a8c4fe`, `-sessionuid` `0x11a8c50c`,
  `-deathmatch` `0x11a8c51a` (reads `[0x1216aedc]`), `-coop` `0x11a8c533` (reads `[0x1216aee4]`),
  `-domination` `0x11a8c54d` (`[0x1216aeec]`), `-headquarters` `0x11a8c567` (`[0x1216aef4]`),
  `-firestorm` `0x11a8c57d` (`[0x1216aefc]`), `-dash` `0x11a8c597` (`[0x1216af04]`), fallback
  `[0x1216af0c]` at `0x11a8c5b1`.
- **Mode-id values (FC3.dll `0x1216aed0 .. 0x1216af10`, byte-identical to FC3_d3d11.dll
  `0x1218ffd0 .. 0x12190010`):** `0x9ba82025` first entry (`0x1216aed0` and `0x1216aed4`),
  `-deathmatch 0x8b40f692`, `-coop 0x43521e6f`, `-domination 0xab4fc8e2`, `-headquarters 0x9991daec`,
  `-firestorm 0xf66ffb87`, `-dash 0x2100ced7`, default `0x6c2b3868`; each id is followed by a
  `0xffffffff` pair word, and an extra id `0xd2fd0a6b` sits just before the block (`0x1216aecc` /
  `0x1218ffcc`). Two corrections to the earlier note: `-deathmatch` reads `0x1216aedc` (not
  `0x1216aed4`), and the id recorded as "unknown" (`0xf66ffb87`) is **`-firestorm`**.
- **The cascade reads the ids, it does not write them:** the values are static `.data`
  initializers present in both 1.06 modules, so `-coop` is a live mode id, not a stripped string.
  `-online` is a separate selector: `[ebp-0xc]` is set to `3` at `0x11a8c5bf` by default and to `2`
  at `0x11a8c5cf` when `-online` matches (consistent with the host-model finding "session type = 3
  without `-online`").
- **Consumer of the cascade id (FC3.dll `0x11a8c5d6 .. 0x11a8c5f0`):** `mov ecx,[ebp+8]` →
  `mov edx,[ebx]` → `mov eax,[edx+0x14]` → **`mov [ebx+0xa4],ecx`** (the mode id lands in object
  field `+0xa4`) → `mov ecx,ebx` / `call eax`, then `mov eax,[edx+0x18]`, `mov ecx,ebx`, `call eax`.
  FC3_d3d11.dll mirrors the chain (`-domination` `0x11aa08ed`, `-headquarters` `0x11aa0907`,
  `-firestorm` `0x11aa091d`, `-dash` `0x11aa0937`, `-online` `0x11aa0959`) and the `+0x14` dispatch
  at `0x11aa0976`.
- **What the cascade function does after the id is set (`FC3.dll 0x11a8c5f0 .. 0x11a8c6b9`,
  `local/scratch/cascade_tail.txt`):** it calls `0x119696d0` (`0x11a8c5f7`) and `0x11a8bdc0`
  (`0x11a8c601`), runs a ref-count block built from `[ebx+0x9c] + 0x4c` and `[ebp+0x14]` (`cmp`
  `0x11a8c612`, `lock xadd [edx], 1` `0x11a8c62d`, `lock xadd [edx], -1` `0x11a8c645`,
  `call 0x100945e0` `0x11a8c64f`), sets a byte flag `[ecx+0xd] = 1` (`0x11a8c668`), reads the string
  global `-sessionuid` `0x11fb81e4` (`0x11a8c696`), then writes launch-record fields
  `[ebx+0x64] = 0xfffffffd` (`0x11a8c69c`), `[ebx+0x40] = eax` (`0x11a8c6a3`), `[ebx+0x44] = edx`
  (`0x11a8c6a6`) and `[ebx+0x74] = 6` (`0x11a8c6b2`). The mode id travels inside a
  vtable-dispatched object; no record is registered from this path.
- **`+0xa4` census (`FC3.dll .text` walk with `local/scratch/probe5.py`, output
  `local/scratch/a4sites.txt` 93637 bytes):** 832 functions touch a `[reg+0xa4]` slot (835 refs) but
  exactly one also touches a mode-id global, `0x1181c440`, and that one turned out to be UI code (next
  bullet). `+0xa4` is not a unique fingerprint of the launch mode, so the consumer search must follow
  the vtable dispatch instead.
- **Dead end: `FC3.dll 0x1181c440` is a FlowGraph UI handler named `customMapEvaluation`.** Its `+0xa4`
  refs are stack slots (`mov [esp+0xa4],4` `0x1181d09e`, `mov [esp+0xa4],0x190` `0x1181d101`); the body
  loads its name `mov esi, 0x1201b27c` (`0x1181c45b`, a UTF-16 string right after the `CFCX` fourcc at
  `0x1201b276`); `scripts/fc3_callers.py` reports `0 E8 callers` plus one absolute data ref at
  `0x1201b444`, which sits in a handler table `0x1201b404 .. 0x1201b458` surrounded by `OnMapPublish`
  `0x1201b380`, `OnEnterMyMapsOK` `0x1201b39c`, `OnEnterMapArchiveOK` `0x1201b3bc`, `ExitMyMaps`
  `0x1201b3e4`, `updateCustomMapEvaluation` `0x1201b478`, `multiCustomMapEvaluationReportContent`
  `0x1201b4b0`, `multiCustomMapEvaluationFavoriteContent` `0x1201b500`,
  `multiCustomMapEvaluationLikeContent` `0x1201b550`, `customMapEvaluationTagsRequest` `0x1201b598`.
- **Registry-shaped loop found inside that handler (`0x1181ceef .. 0x1181cf30`), kept because it is the
  pattern a real session registry would show** (listing saved to `local/scratch/registry_scan_loop.txt`):
  `mov ecx,[0x128048c0]` (`0x1181ceef`), `mov edi,[edx+0xc4]` (`0x1181cef5`), `mov eax,[ecx+8]`
  (`0x1181cefb`), `mov edx,[ecx+4]` (`0x1181cefe`), `lea edx,[edx+eax*4]` (`0x1181cf01`),
  `mov eax,[ecx+4]` (`0x1181cf04`), `cmp eax,edx` (`0x1181cf07`), `je 0x1181cf21` (`0x1181cf09`),
  then the scan `mov ebx,[eax]` (`0x1181cf11`) → `cmp dword ptr [ebx+0x20], esi` (`0x1181cf13`) →
  `je 0x1181cf1f` (`0x1181cf16`) on a match, `add eax,4` (`0x1181cf18`) → `cmp eax,edx`
  (`0x1181cf1b`) → `jne 0x1181cf11` (`0x1181cf1d`) on a miss; `xor ebx,ebx` (`0x1181cf1f`) is reached
  from both the match jump and the exhausted loop, and `0x1181cf21` (`mov edx,[ecx+8]`,
  `mov ecx,[ecx+4]`, `lea edx,[ecx+edx*4]`, `cmp eax,edx`, `je 0x1181cf35`, `mov eax,[eax]`) starts a
  second scan of the same container. `esi = [0x1216aeec]` (`-domination 0xab4fc8e2`, read at
  `0x1181cf0b`), `edi = [0x1216af0c]` (default `0x6c2b3868`, read at `0x1181ce50`), and the field
  compared on the other side is `[edx+0xc4]`.
 New container: **`[0x128048c0]`**, `+4` = count, `+8` = index base scaled ×4, entries
  are pointers whose field `+0x20` holds an id — distinct from the execLines container `[0x12829424]`.
  Open: what `[0x128048c0]` is, and whether the cascade's `+0xa4` value is ever matched against an
  entry's `+0x20`.
- **Consumers of the launch-mode id located** (whole-`.text` scan of every instruction that reads a
  mode-id global, dump in `local/scratch/modeid_sites.txt`: only 3 functions read them in `FC3.dll`):
  `0x1111e390` (`cmp eax,[0x1216aee4]` at `0x1111e4e3`), `0x1111e860` (`cmp eax,[0x1216aee4]` at
  `0x1111e91e`), `0x1181c440` (default id at `0x1181ce50`, `-domination` at `0x1181cf0b`). In
  `0x1111e390`: `mov eax,[edi+0x10]` (`0x1111e399`) holds the mode id, `mov ecx,[ebx+0x154]`
  (`0x1111e3bc`) is the container it searches, `call 0x10260770` (`0x1111e3c7`) is the id→record lookup
  (`cmp byte ptr [ecx+0x5c],0` `0x10260773`, `cmp dword ptr [ecx+0x54],0` `0x1026077b`,
  `mov eax,[ecx+0x50]` `0x10260781`, `call 0x1025fee0` `0x1026078a`, `cmp dword ptr [eax+4],2`
  `0x1026079a`, `cmp ecx,[edi+0xc]` `0x102607a3`, returns the record or 0), and the record's field
  `+0xcc` (`0x1111e3d8` `mov eax,[eax+0xcc]`) yields the container the record belongs to.
- **The coop test reads through a second singleton (`FC3.dll 0x1111e4ce .. 0x1111e517`)**:
  `mov edx,[0x12804acc]` (`0x1111e4ce`) → `mov eax,[edx+0x68]` (`0x1111e4d4`) → `mov eax,[eax+0x30]`
  (`0x1111e4db`) → `or eax,0xffffffff` when the accessor is null (`0x1111e4e0`) → `cmp eax,[0x1216aee4]`
  (`0x1111e4e3`) → `mov ecx,[ebx+0x154]` (`0x1111e4ef`) → `mov eax,[ecx]` → `mov eax,[eax+0x68]` →
  `call eax` with `[esp+0x18]` (`0x1111e4fe`) → `mov eax,[edx+0x24]` (`0x1111e508`) → `call eax`
  (`0x1111e50b`) → `test al,al` → `cmp byte ptr [esp+0xb],al` → `cmp dword ptr [0x12835c94],0`
  (`0x1111e517`). New globals: **`[0x12804acc]`** (accessor at `+0x68`, vtable slots `+0x30` and `+0x24`)
  and **`[0x12835c94]`**.
- **The same coop test appears a second time inside `0x1111e860` (`FC3.dll 0x1111e860 .. 0x1111ecb0`,
  dump `local/scratch/r1_out.txt`)**: `mov eax,[0x12804acc]` (`0x1111e902`) → `mov eax,[eax+0x68]`
  (`0x1111e907`) → `mov eax,[eax+0x30]` (`0x1111e916`) / `or eax,0xffffffff` (`0x1111e91b`) →
  `cmp eax,[0x1216aee4]` (`0x1111e91e`) → `sete al` (`0x1111e924`) → `mov byte ptr [esp+0x13],al`
  (`0x1111e927`) → `test al,al` → `jne 0x1111e98e` (`0x1111e92d`); the negative branch tests
  `cmp dword ptr [ecx+0xc],0` (`0x1111e932`), `cmp dword ptr [0x12835c94],0` (`0x1111e938`),
  `call 0x10003cd0` (`0x1111e94a`), `call 0x10002aa0` (`0x1111e961`), `call 0x10425d20`
  (`0x1111e96e`, `cmp eax,ebx` → `je 0x1111e98e`), `mov ecx,[0x12805434]` (`0x1111e97f`),
  `call 0x11363ab0` (`0x1111e985`). Shared shape of both functions: `mov ebx,[ebp+8]` + `add ebx,0x108`
  (`0x1111e8ad`) or `mov ecx,[ebx+0x154]` (`0x1111e3bc`, `0x1111e437`) as the searched container,
  `call 0x10425d20` (`0x1111e8b8`, `0x1111e96e`) with the `cmp eax,ebx` end-of-container sentinel, entry
  field read `mov eax,[eax+0x20]` (`0x1111e8c5` — the same `+0x20` id field as the `[0x128048c0]`
  entries), `mov ecx,[0x12805434]` (`0x1111e8c8`), `call 0x11363b10` (`0x1111e8de`),
  `cmp dword ptr [ebp+0x14],2` (`0x1111e98e`) and `cmp dword ptr [ebp+0x14],1` (`0x1111ea44`) as the
  mode selector, `call 0x104245c0` (`0x1111e9a5`), and the final gate `mov ecx,[0x12805180]`
  (`0x1111ea72`) → `mov eax,[ecx+4]` (`0x1111ea78`) → `mov edx,[eax+0x11c]` (`0x1111ea7b`) →
  `mov ecx,[eax+0x118]` (`0x1111ea81`) → `push edx; push ecx; push eax` → `call 0x102000d0`
  (`0x1111ea8a`) → `test al,al` → `je 0x1111eb48` (`0x1111ea91`). Open: what `[0x12805180]`,
  `[0x12805434]`, `0x102000d0`, `0x10425d20` are, and who calls `0x1111e390`/`0x1111e860`.
- **Container `[0x128048c0]` owner (`local/scratch/container_sites.txt`, 13 functions)**: creator
  `0x10048230` publishes it twice (`0x10048289`, `0x10048297 mov dword ptr [0x128048c0], eax`); readers
  `0x10f0c770` `0x10f0c7ca`, `0x10f0ea50` `0x10f0ec39`, `0x10f0ed30` `0x10f0ef19`, `0x113224c0`
  `0x11322514`, `0x113235d0` `0x113236a9`, `0x11340560` `0x113408f8` + `0x11340bdb`, `0x11709440`
  `0x11709450`, `0x1181c440`, `0x118227d0` `0x11822875`, `0x119288b0` `0x11928dee`, `0x11997720`
  `0x11997723` (that read is the function's first instruction, so it looks like a getter). Creator
  anatomy: guards `cmp byte ptr [0x128042fc], bl` (`0x1004823a`) and `cmp byte ptr [0x12162beb], bl`
  (`0x10048247`), `call 0x11789a00` (`0x10048242`), 0x20-byte allocation `mov edx,0x10` /+ `lea
  ecx,[edx+0x10]` / `call 0x1177f7c0` (`0x1004824f`, `0x10048254`, `0x10048257`), vtable `mov dword ptr
  [eax], 0x11f224c4` (`0x10048271`), fields `+4 +8 +0xc +0x10 +0x14 +0x18` zeroed.
- **`[0x128048c0]` vtable `0x11f224c4` gates its slots on flag bits of `+0x10`** (dump in
  `local/scratch/probe8.py` output): slot 1 `0x100a5df0` (`test byte ptr [ecx+0x10],1` `0x100a5df3`,
  `mov esi,[ecx+0x14]`, `add esi,[ebp+8]`, `cmp dword ptr [ecx+8],-1`, `lea eax,[ecx+4]`,
  `mov eax,[edx+0x20]` → `call eax` at `0x100a5e14`), slot 2 `0x100a5e30` (bit 2), slot 3 `0x100a5e70`
  (bit 4); slot 0 `0x100a5b10` bounds-checks `esi` against `[0x121bbff8]` (`0x100a5b25`) and
  `[0x121a7fdc]` (`0x100a5b2d`) after `call 0x117097d0`.
- **Mode ids occur in three other FC3.dll data tables** (the next candidates for the
  session-descriptor registry): `0x1216aa2c .. 0x1216aa44` (`-deathmatch 0x1216aa2c`, default
  `0x1216aa30`, `-firestorm 0x1216aa34`, `-domination 0x1216aa38`, `-headquarters 0x1216aa3c`,
  `-coop 0x1216aa40`, `-dash 0x1216aa44`); `0x12173ff0 .. 0x1217400c` (`-domination 0x12173ff0`,
  `-coop 0x12173ff4`, `-deathmatch 0x12173ff8`, `-headquarters 0x12174000`, `-firestorm 0x12174008`,

  sites whose stored value points at it, and FC3_d3d11.dll `0x1218ffe4` has the same **30**; the mode-name
  string has **2** in each module (FC3.dll `0x11fb81fc` -> `0x11a8c534`, `0x11a8c95d`; FC3_d3d11.dll
  `0x11fdc0cc` -> `0x11aa08d4`, `0x11aa0cfd`). FC3.dll accessor base `0x1282f6cc` has **4** HIGHLOW sites

- **Lesson (2026-11-05): the mode ids are not `hash(name)`.** Recomputing `-deathmatch`, `-coop`,
  `-domination`, `-headquarters`, `-firestorm`, `-dash` and the default mode name with FNV-1a, FNV-1,
  djb2, sdbm, ELF and CRC32 (in the 26 name variants already tested) gave **0** matches in either module,
  so the ids are constants in a game table, not values derived from the flag strings.
- **Lesson (2026-11-05): `scripts/fc3_reloc_xref.py` code mode cannot see `push imm32`.** For the two
  `push <mode-name VA>` sites inside the cascade body, code mode printed `verified instructions: 0` and
  `rejected (no instruction ends at the field): 2`, while `--mode reloc` found both sites: for
  `push imm32` arguments only the reloc mode is reliable.

- Renaming `bin/upc_r1_loader.dll` does not free the stub: the game exits silently ~25 s after
  launch (no window, no WER event) — the Ubisoft Connect loader is a hard start-up dependency.
- `HKLM` writes are not available to the current user (not elevated), so the registry cannot be
  redirected to a private copy. `local/registry-fc3-backup.reg` holds the original values.

  -> NOPs) lets two copies run side by side; the Uplay party-list crash patch is 1.05-only (the
  pattern does not occur in 1.06, new UPC API).

- FC3.dll 1.06 carries the SAME switch machinery, at its own addresses (verified by byte scan of the
  little-endian string VA in `.text`) (VA = file offset + 0xc00 for this binary's `.text`): the launcher
  function spans `0x11a8cf90`..`0x11a8d103`; `-host` `0x11f0e6ac` is referenced at VAs `0x117a6139`,
  `0x117a6794`, `0x11a8c90c`, `0x11a8cf07`, `0x11a8f657`; `-client` `0x11f0e748` at `0x117a67a2`,
  `0x11a8cfc3`, `0x11a8f67f`; `-disableplayersetuponquicklaunch` `0x11fb7d28` at `0x11a8c3b0`
  and `0x11a8e624`. The bytes at those sites are the push immediates (`ac e6 f0 11` / `28 7d fb 11`). FC3.dll's strstr import slot is
  `[0x11bcb5c0] = MSVCR100.dll:strstr`, used by the validity helper `0x11a8b8c0` which pushes
  `-online` `0x11fb8234`, `-login` `0x11fb8170` and the error string `'Missing -login or -online for
  -quickmatch.'` `0x11fb82d8` → the `-quickmatch` handler validates that `-login`+`-online` are present.
  **This confirms FC3.dll itself contains the multi-player launch-argument validation layer, and the
  `-quickmatch` route requires `-login` + `-online`.** In `-quickmatch`/`-host` handling
  (`0x11a8d0c5`..`0x11a8d0eb`): `lea edx,[ebp+8]; call 0x11341dc0` (returns a session-interface object),
  `mov ecx,[eax]; mov eax,[ecx]; mov eax,[eax+0x74]; push edx; call eax` — a **vftable slot 0x74** call on
  that object with an out-parameter `[ebp+0x10]`, then `call 0x10005570` (destructor) and `jmp 0x11a8cf90`.
  The failure path calls `0x11343640`, a destructor that writes the vftable `0x11f90644` (same page as the
  session-operation field strings `execLines` `0x11f90af0`) and frees the fields at `[obj+0x98]`, `[obj+0x9c]`
  ⇒ the object IS the session object. So the session-creation call is **vftable slot 0x74** of the object
  returned by `0x11341dc0` — the exact target a quick-launch/`-exec` route must reach.
  The collected console lines are `gfx_NoLaunchCountdown 1` `0x11fdbc60`, `gfx_NoPostRoundCountdown 1`
  `0x11fdbc78`, `gfx_NoPreRoundTimeOut 1` `0x11fdbc94`, `multi_DisableInitialSpawnDelay 1` `0x11fdbcac`,
  `hack_loadOutIsWaitingForMissionBlocks 0` `0x11fdbcd0`, plus switch-driven extras `starMatch_with_debugCameras 1`
  `0x11fdbd10` + `multi_SkipIntroMovie 3` `0x11fdbd30` (from `-debugFramerateCameras` `0x11fdbcf8`) and
  `cheat_GodMode 2` `0x11fdbd8c` (when `-demoType` `0x11fdbd80` equals `StageDemo` `0x11f9a8a0`, compared
  with `MSVCR100.dll:_stricmp` `[0x11be1390]` at `0x11aa3741`).
- **Helper identification (corrects this file's earlier claim that `0x10002a50` was a console executor).**
  Disassembling the callees: `0x10002a50` = **strlen** (aligned zero-byte scan `lea esi,[ecx+0x7efefeff]`
  … `test esi,0x81010100` … `sub eax,edx; ret`), `0x10001ec0` = SSO `std::string` assign (`add ecx,0xc;`
  `call 0x10700c10` memmove into the inline buffer, then `[obj+4] = len`, `[obj+0xc+len] = 0`),
  `0x10037dc0` = aligned alloc/resize helper, `0x10174710` = heap free, `0x11788480` / `0x11790540` =
  `std::string` bookkeeping that write the class pointer `0x128ac3f4` into the global object
  `0x12829424` (see the singleton bullet; `0x128ac450` was read from the same kind of site and is likewise
  a `.bss` object, not a file constant). `0x11a1b940` (60 call sites) = "find `<switch>` in the command line, take the text
  after it, store it in an SSO string": two `strstr` calls (`[0x11be1618]`) + `strlen` + assign, with `"`
  (byte `0x22`) quote handling and a space (`0x20`, table at `0x11f31df8`) token split via `0x10003f40`.
  ⇒ `-exec` / `-execline` values are **collected** into the execLines container (`0x12829424`), not executed
  in the launcher. The execution step is the engine's execLines consumer (`FC3.dll` session field
  `execLines` `0x11f90af0`, registered at `0x113429ca`) — the next probe target.
- **Container-global reference count and construction sites (verified 2026-11-02).** A little-endian scan of
  `FC3_d3d11.dll .text` for the byte pattern `24 94 82 12` finds **17 332** references to the global
  `0x12829424`, confirming it is a real singleton used throughout the launcher. Exactly two sites write an
  immediate class pointer into it: `c7 05 24 94 82 12 f4 c3 8a 12` at `0x1178846e` and again at `0x118e82fd`
  (value `0x128ac3f4`, i.e. `f4 c3 8a 12` little-endian). All other 17 330 references are reads
  (`8b/89/8d/83/81 … [0x12829424]`, `ff 35`), i.e. normal member access of one global object.

  `DeleteMessageId` `0x11f90bd0`, `PlaylistId` `0x11f90be0`, `MatchMakingEnabled` `0x11f90bec`,
  `SessionRefId` `0x11f90c00`, `SessionType` `0x11f90c10`, `NetEngineType` `0x11f90c1c`,
  `AccountType` `0x11f90c2c`, `SearchRanked` `0x11f90c38`, `SearchRetries` `0x11f90c48`,
